No production mutation, Dedicated compute spend, duplicate agent creation, or balance consumption is allowed without its recorded gate.15:Tde2,## Summary
CameraWeb.startRecording() opens a SEPARATE microphone MediaStream via getUserMedia({audio:true}) and merges its audio tracks into a new `streamToRecord`, but it never stores that audio stream. stopRecording() only calls mediaRecorder.stop() and stopPreview() only stops `this.mediaStream.getTracks()` (video-only). The audio MediaStreamTrack is therefore never stop()ed, so the microphone stays live after recording ends and even after the preview is fully torn down. On real browsers/Electron this keeps the OS microphone-in-use indicator lit and holds the mic device open — a privacy-visible resource leak. Repeated start/stop cycles accumulate live mic tracks.

## Reproduction
```
Added a jsdom probe test in src/probe.test.ts (removed after) that mocks navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia to return a video-only stream for the preview and a separate audio-only stream (whose audio track has a stop() spy) for the {audio:true} recording request, plus a fake MediaRecorder/MediaStream and a video `src` setter that fires onloadedmetadata so stopRecording() resolves. Sequence: startPreview() -> startRecording({audio:true}) -> stopRecording() -> stopPreview(). Ran `bunx vitest run --config vitest.config.ts src/probe.test.ts`. Result: `expect(videoTrack.stop).toHaveBeenCalled()` passes but `expect(audioTrack.stop).toHaveBeenCalled()` FAILS ("expected vi.fn() to be called at least once"), proving the microphone track is never stopped. Expected: the microphone track opened for recording is stopped on teardown.
```

## Proposed fix
Store the audio stream on a private field (e.g. `this.recordingAudioStream`) when startRecording opens it, then stop and clear its tracks in stopRecording()'s onstop handler (and defensively in stopPreview) alongside the existing teardown. Keep the merge behavior identical; only add lifecycle cleanup. Test plan (>=10 lines, jsdom): (1) start preview + start recording with audio, assert the mic track is stopped after stopRecording(); (2) recording with audio:false does not open a mic stream; (3) start/stop two recording cycles and assert no mic track remains live; (4) stopPreview() while recording also stops the mic track. Reuse the fake MediaRecorder/MediaStream/getUserMedia harness from the probe.

## Evidence plan
PR will include the failing-then-passing vitest output for the new lifecycle tests, and a before/after showing the audio track stop() spy call count (0 -> >=1). Since this is the web fallback, evidence is the deterministic jsdom test asserting track.stop() is invoked on the microphone track across stopRecording and stopPreview paths.

## Duplicate search
gh search issues/prs on elizaOS/eliza (queries: "camera microphone track leak", "camera audio track stop recording", "capacitor-camera microphone", "startRecording microphone leak", prs "camera microphone track") — all returned zero results. No open or closed report or in-flight fix.

_Filed under a standing operator authorization for the autonomous audit pipeline, after local reproduction and duplicate search._

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"anthropic","model":"claude-opus-4-8","client":"pi-coding-agent","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@f809c5d04baca03571418a2543d291f903c42f4b:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->16:T11ab,## Summary
`defaultSpawn` in plugins/plugin-cli-inference/src/claude-cli.ts (shared by both the Claude and Codex production inference paths via codex-cli-exec.ts) opens the required `/dev/null` stdin file descriptor with `openSync(opts.stdinPath, "r")` and passes the raw fd into `spawn(...)` stdio, but never closes the parent's copy of that fd. Node dups the fd into the child at spawn time; the parent's descriptor stays open forever. Every `ClaudeCli.generate()` / `CodexCli.exec()` call — i.e. every large-tier chat/route/synthesis inference turn when ELIZA_CHAT_VIA_CLI is enabled — leaks exactly one fd. On a long-running agent this monotonically grows the process fd table until it hits the OS limit and subsequent spawns/opens fail with EMFILE, taking down the inference route (and anything else needing an fd). This is the core inference data path, so the leak is unbounded in normal operation, not an edge case.

## Reproduction
```
Mechanism proven with a standalone reproduction that mirrors defaultSpawn's exact stdio handling (openSync + spawn with the fd, detached, no close):
  /tmp/fdtest.mjs: opens /dev/null via openSync, spawns a child with stdio:[fd,'pipe','pipe'], never closes fd; loops 20 times counting /proc/self/fd.
  Observed: "fds before: 21" -> "fds after 20 spawns: 42" (one fd leaked per call).
Control with the fix (closeSync(fd) after spawn), /tmp/fdtest2.mjs and fdtest3.mjs:
  Observed: "fds after 20 spawns (with closeSync): 22" (flat), and child stdin still functions — child reads "EOF:0" from /dev/null, so closing the parent copy immediately after spawn does not break the child's stdin.
Expected per the file's own contract ("`/dev/null` stdin is REQUIRED"): the fd is a per-call resource that must be released; observed: it is never closed, so N inference calls hold N open fds.
```

## Proposed fix
In defaultSpawn (plugins/plugin-cli-inference/src/claude-cli.ts), close the parent's copy of stdinFd once the child has inherited it. Since libuv dups the fd synchronously during spawn, add `try { closeSync(stdinFd); } catch {}` immediately after the `spawn(...)` call returns (import closeSync from node:fs). This releases the descriptor for both the success and error/timeout paths without touching the child's stdin (verified: child still sees /dev/null EOF). Scope is ~2 lines plus the import; both CLI variants share this spawner so a single fix covers claude-cli and codex-cli-exec. Test plan (>=10 real lines) added to __tests__/cli-inference.test.ts alongside the existing real-spawn "defaultSpawn timeout escalation" block: a test that snapshots readdirSync('/proc/self/fd').length, runs defaultSpawn against a trivial real child (process.execPath -e 'process.exit(0)') ~15 times with stdinPath '/dev/null', and asserts the post-run fd count has not grown by more than a small constant (proving no per-call leak). Gate the assertion on process.platform === 'linux' (procfs) or use a portable fd probe; the existing suite already spawns real children here so no new harness is required.

## Evidence plan
The PR will attach: (1) the new failing-before/passing-after fd-count test output from `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-cli-inference test`; (2) a before/after /proc/self/fd count from a loop of real defaultSpawn calls showing linear growth pre-fix and flat post-fix; (3) confirmation the child stdin still receives EOF from /dev/null after the immediate close (child prints EOF marker), proving the fix does not regress the "stdin REQUIRED" behavior the file documents.

## Duplicate search
gh search issues --repo elizaOS/eliza --state all "cli-inference file descriptor" -> empty; "openSync stdin leak spawn" -> empty; "file descriptor leak" -> empty. gh search prs --repo elizaOS/eliza --state all "defaultSpawn stdin fd leak" -> empty; "plugin-cli-inference spawn" -> empty. No open or closed issue/PR references this fd leak.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"anthropic","model":"claude-opus-4-8","client":"pi-coding-agent","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@a935a4c278f6de26c570c19cca7d4f9e1f77f65a:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->17:T156f,## Summary
SwabbleWeb.start() (Web Speech fallback in plugins/plugin-native-swabble/src/web.ts) assigns this.recognition, then `await`s startAudioLevelMonitoring() (which itself awaits getUserMedia) BEFORE calling recognition.start(). getUserMedia is always asynchronous, so any stop() that runs during that await interleaves: stop() sets isActive=false, calls this.recognition.stop() (a no-op, recognition not started yet), sets this.recognition=null, and calls stopAudioLevelMonitoring() while the level-meter stream/interval do not yet exist. When start() then resumes it creates this.mediaStream + a 100ms setInterval and calls recognition.start(), whose onstart sets isActive=true and re-emits state "listening". The net result after a start()+stop() sequence: the microphone MediaStream stays live (browser mic indicator on — privacy leak), the audioLevel interval fires forever, and the SpeechRecognition instance keeps running and auto-restarting via onend (isActive===true) — yet this.recognition is null, so no later stop() can ever free it. isListening() reports {listening:true} after the consumer already called stop(). This is a state-machine/teardown-survival race distinct from the fatal-recognition-error leak fixed by merged PR #22656 (that fix only tears down on onerror/onend, neither of which fires here).

## Reproduction
```
In plugins/plugin-native-swabble (node_modules present) I added a probe vitest reusing the file's FakeRecognition harness plus minimal AudioContext/analyser mocks and a getUserMedia returning a deferred promise wrapping a stream whose track.stop() is spied. Sequence: const p = plugin.start({config:{triggers:["eliza"]}}); await Promise.resolve(); await plugin.stop(); resolveGum(stream); await p. Then assert plugin.isListening() === {listening:false}, FakeRecognition.latest.running === false, and track.stopped === true. Observed via `./node_modules/.bin/vitest run src/probe-race.test.ts`: `AssertionError: expected { listening: true } to deeply equal { listening: false }` — the plugin remains listening with recognition.running true and the mic track never stopped, after the consumer already called stop(). Expected: a start()+stop() sequence must leave the plugin fully torn down (not listening, recognition stopped, mic released). Probe file removed after the run; `git status --short plugins/` is clean.
```

## Proposed fix
In start()'s Web Speech branch, guard against a stop() that raced the getUserMedia await. Capture the intended recognition instance before the await and, after `await this.startAudioLevelMonitoring()`, detect supersession: `if (this.recognition !== recognition) { this.stopAudioLevelMonitoring(); return { started: false }; }` before calling recognition.start(). This releases the level-meter stream/interval that startAudioLevelMonitoring created during the await and avoids starting an unreferenceable recognizer. (Equivalently, a monotonically increasing startToken/`stopping` flag set by stop() and checked after the await.) Scope is ~4 lines in one file, no contract change. Test plan (>=10 lines, extends existing web.test.ts harness): (1) race teardown — deferred getUserMedia, call start() then stop() before resolving, resolve the stream, assert isListening() false, the FakeRecognition never left running, and the mic track.stop spy was called; (2) normal path preserved — start() fully awaited then a valid final speech result still emits transcript/wakeWord and isListening() true; (3) idempotency — a second stop() after the race does not throw. All assertions run against the real SwabbleWeb, no stubbing of the unit under test.

## Evidence plan

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"anthropic","model":"claude-opus-4-8","client":"pi-coding-agent","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@a7824607875cc9474b83cbec28a6ba3149dcfdb7:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->18:Tbc5,## Defect

Managed Cloud routes can mount the tab/view application while private surface registration is still pending. The wildcard `/cloud/*` bootstrap waits for readiness, but an individually registered route with `group: "cloud"` or `group: "admin"` takes a different branch through `CloudRouteElement` and bypasses that readiness check.

The production loader publishes individual domain routes before later imports register the app-shell page that owns `/cloud/*`. A cold Billing payment-detail link can therefore reach the application before its owning page is available.

## Reproduction and evidence

- The relevant production files are unchanged between the repair branch and freshly fetched `develop@9109339b01d068d5e5f0d42f6b15bdd8aa4ed269`.
- A mounted `CloudRouterShell` regression uses two explicit latches: publish `cloud/billing/payments/:id` during the private load, then complete that load separately. No timer or fake route implementation replaces the shell under test.
- The existing three tests pass. The new test fails both with the complete file and in isolation: registration remains `pending`, yet the application probe is mounted.
- Exact command (Bun 1.3.14, Node 24.15.0): `bun run --cwd packages/ui test src/cloud/shell/CloudRouterShell.private-registration.test.tsx -t 'keeps a newly registered Billing route behind the pending private-registration barrier' --reporter=verbose`.
- This was found while diagnosing the real local Billing matrix: its second cold payment-detail navigation timed out with the launcher visible and no request to that detail API. The deterministic regression proves the readiness defect; it does not yet prove that this defect alone explains the entire E2E failure.

## Acceptance

- Individually registered managed Cloud routes honor the same pending/error/retry/ready boundary as the wildcard bootstrap.
- Publishing a route during pending never mounts the application early; successful completion preserves the requested deep link.
- Failure after partial registration shows the existing unavailable/retry state without mounting the application; a successful retry recovers.
- Public routes, session authorization, and canonical-host redirects retain their existing behavior.
- Re-run the affected routing tests and the real local Billing UI matrix, then the required app visual checks on the final branch.

## Coordination

CLAIMING: bounded readiness-boundary repair discovered during the #22966 / #26752 child repair. Production scope is the existing Cloud router shell; tests stay with that owner. I will keep this in a separate commit, preserve the contributor branch, and coordinate the final PR base before publication.

Read-only inventory across all currently open PRs found no overlapping changes in the affected route/registration files. #28784 is a separate apex `/chat` redirect change, not this readiness contract. No provider call, payment, deployment, migration, production/staging mutation, or authorization-policy change is included.

21:T173d,Closes #29117

## Summary

- recover built-in app routes across deferred runtime boot instead of caching an early 404 as permanent unavailability
- route Settings, Wallet, Calendar, Notes, Browser, Knowledge, Memories, and Trajectories through a shared responsive workspace/layout system
- scope view caches and reloads to the active agent authority so switching between local, Shared, and Dedicated agents cannot leak stale capability state
- remount Knowledge, Memories, and Trajectories at the authority boundary and cancel delayed or in-flight warm-up work so an agent switch cannot render, request, or cache the prior agent's data
- make deferred readiness route-specific: a live runtime route or direct host handler remains reachable even if unrelated late boot work is still pending or fails
- keep Android's build-pinned remote target fail-closed even when a pre-built UI constructs the shared client before the app publishes its compiled target; stale Cloud sockets, bases, and bearers are retired before bootstrap awaits
- expose the active agent's EVM and Solana public addresses in Wallet, with the EVM address shared across every supported EVM chain
- make Shared-agent capability boundaries explicit and typed instead of rendering broken or retry-looping views
- consolidate Settings navigation, rows, controls, connector states, error states, and mobile/desktop framing onto reusable UI primitives
- preserve overlay-aware geometry and chat clearance across Browser, Pendant, Experience, Files, fullscreen plugin pages, and ambient Cloud routes

## Runtime capability boundary

| View | Local / Dedicated | Shared |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Wallet public addresses | Supported | Supported through the Steward wallet proxy |
| Memories | Supported | Supported through the durable Shared-memory adapter |
| Permissions read | Supported | Returns the honest empty capability set |
| Browser | Supported where the native browser surface exists | Typed non-retryable Dedicated-required state |
| Calendar | Supported | Typed non-retryable Dedicated-required state |
| Notes | Supported | Typed non-retryable Dedicated-required state |
| Knowledge / Documents | Supported | Typed non-retryable Dedicated-required state |
| Trajectories | Supported | Typed non-retryable Dedicated-required state |

This PR does not pretend that isolated plugin runtimes exist on Shared agents. It fixes false cold-boot unavailability and presents true runtime boundaries clearly.

## What this changes

- adds a build-time design dependency graph across tokens, atoms, inferred molecules, declared organisms, and page shells
- fails closed when a reusable molecule or any reachable helper claims raw paint or interaction capability
- keeps an exact zero-entry debt ledger, so new findings fail immediately and stale debt also fails in tight mode
- migrates shared UI and plugin compositions to canonical atoms and typed variants
- adds deterministic molecule ownership, consumer, rendered-tag, and atomic-dependency contracts
- classifies the startup lifecycle as an explicit organism instead of misidentifying it as a reusable molecule
- fixes the short-landscape computer-session layout overlap found during visual audit
- restores the launcher icon smoked-glass contrast through semantic tokens owned by the canonical Card atom
- preserves wallpaper imagery and legibility with a canonical dark scrim contract shared by wallpaper previews, slash-command overlays, and the voice HUD

# Risks

Medium-low. This changes ordinary local-development routing and sign-in behavior, but not deployed production behavior. Explicit production, offline, and coherent self-hosted targets remain available. The highest-risk cases—credential leakage between targets and reuse of a shared dev server with the wrong Cloud profile—are fail-closed and covered by focused tests.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Makes ordinary `bun run dev`, `dev:ui`, package `dev`, and `dev:shared` sessions target staging Cloud by default.
- Centralizes staging, production, offline, and self-hosted target resolution with CLI-over-env precedence and canonical mixed-target rejection.
- Adds explicit `--cloud-target=staging|production|offline` / `ELIZA_DEV_CLOUD_TARGET` overrides plus `dev:local` shortcuts.
- Clears inherited Cloud activation credentials for implicit staging and offline runs, while preserving credentials for explicit and coherent self-hosted targets.
- Fingerprints every public Cloud routing field before reusing a shared local server, without retaining credentials in the registry.
- Sends loopback staging sign-in through the local same-tab `/login` bridge, including mobile, while preserving production/custom device-code behavior.
- Adds deterministic staging and local Playwright lanes with isolated artifacts and CI coverage.
- Documents the local Cloud contract and environment overrides.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and local developer-experience improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

The documentation change is included: package README, `.env.example`, and paired package guidance now describe staging-default development and explicit overrides.

# Risks

Medium. This changes conversation-history reconciliation, where an incorrect lineage decision could drop or duplicate a recently completed turn. Coverage exercises the stale-history race, terminal rekey, and durable convergence, asserting exactly one user row and one assistant row.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Preserves locally owned optimistic message lineage after terminal receipts replace temporary row IDs. A stale independently running history request can therefore merge without deleting the just-rekeyed user and assistant rows; later durable history still converges without duplicates.

The source fix is deliberately narrow: local ownership now follows a temporary `id` **or** a preserved temporary `clientRenderId`. Conversation ownership and server-ID deduplication remain unchanged. The PR also extends the deterministic browser regression fixture and its CI workflow coverage.

Provenance reviewed:

- #26176 and its reconciliation regression coverage: @NubsCarson / nubs;
- the original conversation-history loader and terminal rekey paths: @Shaw.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is needed. The browser fixture and regression tests document the contract directly.

# Risks

Medium. This is a broad, net-negative consolidation across shared contracts, Cloud Worker boundaries, plugin utilities, and UI composition. Public exports, dynamic manifests, package builds, real SQL drivers, Miniflare, Wrangler dry-run, UI contract graphs, and the full repository verification gate cover those boundaries.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Consolidates repeated SQL, connector credential, cloud lifecycle, cryptographic/canonical JSON, and mobile runner implementations under their existing canonical owners.
- Removes obsolete widgets, unused UI elements/layouts, and plugin-local visual implementations where `@elizaos/ui` already owns the component.
- Replaces the broad Cloud Worker Core mirror with the narrow `@elizaos/core/edge` leaf and a fail-loud documents trap.
- Refreshes the repository UI molecular inventory: 889 maintained React files, 100 recognized molecular-role exports, and 12 final dispositions.
- Adds reuse-first guidance to `CONTRIBUTING.md`, paired `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` guides, and applicable skills so contributors search for and extend canonical owners before creating parallel code.
- Adds the two English account-switch fallbacks referenced by the newest `develop` head, which otherwise failed `check:i18n`.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvement/refactor with bug fixes and documentation updates. No intentional breaking public API change.

## Reuse research

# Risks

Medium. Adds a new core service (`InstallationLifecycleService`) and connector contribution surface, but all wiring is opt-in: the Discord adapter registers the contribution and fences message traffic, while every other surface is untouched. Traffic fence is deliberately fail-open for scopes with no installation record (existing installs keep working); only terminal-state records (`removed`/`revoked`/`failed`) stop traffic, which is exactly the issue criterion ("removal stops traffic"). Scope-creep guard: no database migration, no API routes, no UI changes.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Implements the first tranche of the canonical invite-to-ready group installation lifecycle (#23107):

1. **Durable provider-neutral state machine** (`packages/core/src/messaging/installation-lifecycle.ts`): an account-scoped installation record joining provider invite, external group, required scopes, single-use expiring owner claim, runtime room binding, readiness state, removal, and reinstall versioning. States: `invite_created → provider_authorized → agent_joined → permissions_verifying → owner_claim_pending → ready`, with terminal `degraded`/`removed`/`revoked`/`failed`. Signed-event normalization: events are tenant-bound, idempotent, replay-safe (terminal receipt replay is a no-op), and epoch-guarded (future `reinstallVersion` epochs rejected; stale epochs rejected; equal-epoch reapply rejects wrong-secret attempts and persists attempt burning to exhaustion).
2. **Connector contribution contract** (`packages/core/src/messaging/installation-contribution.ts` + `installation-service.ts`): the contract a connector implements — install/deep-link shape, group types, scope requirements, signed event normalization, readiness proof, uninstall, provider-specific activation. `InstallationLifecycleService` registers contributions per `(agentId, connectorId)`.
3. **Discord pilot adapter** (`plugins/plugin-discord/installation-adapter.ts`, `discord-events.ts`, `index.ts`, `messages.ts`): canonical permission catalog (tiered invite URL activation), guildCreate/guildDelete normalization (unknown shapes refused), owner-claim handoff that never exposes tokens in the group, and the gateway-boundary fence — a fenced (removed/revoked/failed) guild skips `handleGuildCreate` at the event-listener seam and stops message traffic at the message-send seam.

## What kind of change is this?

Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation (contract is documented in code headers; user-facing install UX arrives with the next tranche's install-URL surface).

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `packages/core/src/messaging/installation-lifecycle.test.ts` — 49 tests over the state machine: legal transitions, replay/idempotency, claim single-use + expiry + attempt burning, epoch guards, removal semantics.
2. `plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/installation-adapter.test.ts` — contribution shape (permission catalog, tiered invite URLs), event normalization (unknown shapes refused), traffic gate semantics.
3. `plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/installation-gateway-boundary.test.ts` — the fence at the real `setupDiscordEventListeners` seam: a fenced guild never reaches `handleGuildCreate`.
4. `plugins/plugin-discord/__tests__/installation-contribution-init.test.ts` — plugin init registers the contribution against the real core service.

# Deploy Notes

No deploy is included or authorized by this Draft PR.2c:T98b,## Summary

Stacks on #28936 and implements the safe metering/control-plane portion of #25518.

- removes real provider credentials from the scenario/harness environment and transports the selected credential plus per-attempt attestation key over a bounded FD3 bootstrap
- exposes only a loopback model proxy and dummy provider credential to the real runtime
- enforces request, conservative input-byte, authoritative output-token, tool-call, response-size, model, route, redirect, and attempt budgets before or during dispatch
- uses endpoint-specific request allowlists to reject hosted tools/contexts, remote URLs, MCP, containers, shell/code execution, search/audio, premium routing, background modes, and multi-choice bypasses
- requires authoritative OpenAI/Anthropic usage receipts and HMAC-attests canonical meter receipts
- verifies exact chronological accepted-envelope prefixes and rejected suffixes, request bytes/hashes, token arithmetic, model identity, and output caps
- records typed failed receipts across the exact three-attempt policy
- adds black-box production FD3 parser tests for malformed/schema-invalid, oversized, held-open/truncated, and valid payloads

## Evidence

- Head: `7f2dd5be968`
- Base: `cbd4c2f8a82` (#28936)
- Six implementation commits replayed patch-identically onto the base; parser coverage is a separate seventh commit
- Independent P0/P1 review: CLEAN
- Live-model meter: 64/64
- Parent + preload network guards: 10/10
- Production FD3 parser: 5/5, 27 assertions
- Parser review rerun: 5/5 in 4.31s
- Biome, guide parity, and diff checks pass
- Deterministic keyless exact lane passed first attempt and 3/3 before the final parser-only extraction

Shared-host subprocess reruns had only timeout/OS `EPERM` teardown failures; no credential, FD, receipt, HMAC, or request-policy assertion regressed. A loaded-host provider timeout case passed alone in 613ms.

## Security boundary

This PR removes direct environment and descriptor inheritance, but it does **not** claim hostile-code certification. Same-UID Linux `/proc/<pid>/environ` inspection and raw socket/network bypass remain possible unless the scenario process runs in the OS sandbox tracked by #26821. Therefore the credentialed GitHub lane must remain disabled until #26821 lands and proves distinct process identity/proc isolation plus network enforcement.

No live-model request was made.

31:T1ca4,## Summary

Closes #23453

Binds the restore drill's authority to the specific archive, authenticates RPO metadata, and scales the isolation proof to linear — through a signed, expiring, single-use restore capability enforced on the server clock at every destructive boundary.

### What changed

- **Archive-bound capability** (`restore-capability.ts`, new): HMAC-signed envelope binding `target_id` + archive sha256 + nonce, mint-time clamped TTL, single-use. The signature covers `issuedAt` so a re-signed grant cannot claim an older minting.
- **Dual-setting target authority**: the disposable target carries `eliza.restore_target_id` + `eliza.restore_capability` twins in `postgresql.auto.conf`; every guarded psql session re-checks both before its first statement, plus a **server-clock expiry check** (millisecond precision) derived from the signed bytes — no local-check/SQL-execution TOCTOU.
- **Claim ordering**: the transactional exclusivity claim persists **only after** the clean-target role-collision check passes; a refused first invocation leaves no claim, so a retry can never masquerade as a same-capability retry.
- **Session-held drill lock**: advisory-lock holder with a proven file handshake; the holder re-proves lock ownership before its hold-sleep (a DO block — a refused or dispossessed holder aborts instead of leaking a 24h `pg_sleep` backend); release terminates the holder backend gated on `pg_locks` proving the pid still holds this exact lock (PID-reuse safe).
- **Ownership handoff**: `--no-owner` dumps restore as the admin, so the drill hands the database, public schema, and every restored relation to the tenant role (`\gexec`, `%I`-quoted); tenant-role connections prove real access.
- **Provision critical section**: `postgresql.auto.conf` read-modify-write is serialized by a **cluster-wide advisory-lock session** (canonicalized onto the `postgres` database — advisory locks are database-local while the file is cluster-wide), replacing the file-lock protocol entirely.
- **RPO from the manifest**: the manifest inside the encrypted, checksummed archive is authoritative; drifted/future-dated sidecar-manifest pairs fail closed.
- **Linear isolation**: every tenant probed as its own role; ACL assertion covers datdba ownership, PUBLIC-denied, no foreign grantees (OID 0 probe).

Adversarial probes (execution, real PG 14.19):
- Lock holder liveness: holder backend present in `pg_locks` (ExclusiveLock, correct objid, own pid) for the whole window; second claimer refused via sentinel + `'drill lock lost before hold'` abort; release drops the lock server-side (count 0).
- Provision lock cluster-wide exclusivity: claimers arriving via different maintenance-database DSNs (canonicalized to `/postgres`) contend on a single lock — exactly one holds, the other refused, release drops it.
- Typecheck: `tsc --strict --noEmit` on the changed sources clean. Biome clean (3 warnings matching the sibling `migrate-with-diagnostics.postgres.test.ts` convention).

### Review

Independently reviewed (RepoPrompt CE, gpt-5.6-sol) over five rounds; final verdict **APPROVE** with zero must-fix items on head `8b25e11b6f` (post-rebase re-verified). Rounds surfaced and fixed: expiry TOCTOU at the claim boundary, raw-PID termination, refusal-path sleep leak, holder CASE type error (`integer`/`void` — killed every holder; found by review, confirmed by execution probe), provision file-lock TOCTOU (replaced wholesale with the advisory-lock session), and database-local lock scoping.

# Risks

Low. Rendering changes are isolated to launcher icons. Catalog hero imagery,
third-party URL icons, navigation, and application behavior remain unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Replaces per-app launcher treatments with one reusable `LauncherAppIcon` system.
- Maps curated first-party apps to official filled Ionicons while preserving
  third-party image icons and deterministic semantic fallbacks.
- Matches the chat composer with a smoked graphite glass plate, continuous
  corners, inset lighting, and off-white glyphs over the existing orange-cloud wallpaper.
- Removes launcher press, scale, transform, and flash animation.
- Keeps catalog hero previews independent from launcher glyph styling.
- Adds the icon-pack comparison lab used to select Ionicons.
- Vendors the scoped Ionicons 8.1.0 assets and ships their complete MIT notice.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and visual improvement (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project-documentation change is needed. The vendored icon source, version,
scope, and license are documented beside the assets and in the shipped third-party notice.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Open `/views` and compare the launcher grid with the chat composer against the
orange-cloud wallpaper. Confirm the plates share one graphite material, glyphs
remain recognizable, and pressing a tile does not animate the icon.

## Detailed testing steps

1. Start the local app and open `http://127.0.0.1:2190/views`.
2. Inspect the launcher at desktop and phone widths.
3. Confirm all first-party destinations use filled Ionicons and all glyphs load.
4. Press/hold several tiles and confirm no scale, transform, flash, or push animation.
5. Open the icon-pack lab at `http://127.0.0.1:4321/launcher-icons.html` to review

# Risks

Medium, isolated to database schema and the not-yet-consumed replacement-attempt repository surface. The migration adds an owner-lifetime append-only authority table, constraints, partial unique indexes, immutability/state-transition triggers, and a composite foreign key to the exact append-only Docker-node occurrence.

The occurrence tuple is `(history id, Docker-node record id, node incarnation)`, rather than the incarnation UUID alone, so node rotation and ABA reuse fail closed. Direct row deletion and truncation remain forbidden. An organization-owned cascade is allowed only for terminal non-restore history; active replacement effects abort the entire organization deletion. Existing restore-backed retention authorities can still reject organization deletion.

Replacement start takes locks in the account-deletion-safe order `organization KEY SHARE -> optional restore lease UPDATE -> sandbox UPDATE -> attempt insert`. This removes the `start: sandbox -> organization FK` / `delete: organization -> sandbox cascade` AB-BA cycle. For restore-backed starts, database time is re-read after the lease lock, after the potentially contended sandbox lock, and after `INSERT ... RETURNING`; expiry at any barrier aborts and rolls back the attempt. Callers must not pre-lock the sandbox before invoking the start transaction.

This PR does not deploy or apply the migration to a live database. If the migration tail changes first, this branch must be renumbered and revalidated before merge.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds the missing SQL migration and journal registration for the already-defined `agent_sandbox_replacement_attempts` schema/repository, then binds every durable replacement locator to one exact Docker-node occurrence.

The table:

- fences duplicate provider effects by active agent and activation generation;
- binds optional restore-lease provenance;
- enforces complete locator and receipt shapes;
- preserves the exact node occurrence across rotation and ABA reuse;
- revalidates and locks the current Docker-node occurrence before placement/adoption;
- starts under the organization FK-parent lock before taking the optional restore lease and sandbox locks;
- retains attempt history for the owner's lifetime;
- permits terminal non-restore erasure only through the owning organization cascade, while unresolved/provider-succeeded effects block that erasure atomically.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature / database migration substrate.

# Documentation changes needed?

No external documentation change is required. The migration, repository contract, and executable tests document the state machine and authority tuple directly; runtime consumption will be reviewed separately.

## Known gaps / failures

Server trajectory time was 1 to 4 seconds while physical end-to-end perception was 15 to 30 seconds; broader phase instrumentation and warm-room optimization remain follow-up work.3a:T15c2,## What this does

Implements the prompt-integrity contract's own stated rule at the one place it was still unowned: **the provider dispatch boundary**. Two features, three commits:

### 1. Typed provider context-overflow boundary (`42ff44a18b5`)

Today a model-facing payload that exceeds the provider's real context limit surfaces as a raw `AI_APICallError` that errors the whole turn. Live incident (Cerebras, 2026-08): a channel-recap turn composed a 202,427-token prompt against the 131,072 limit — the turn died, the trajectory recorded `errored`, the user got silence.

Per the doctrine consolidated in #26780 (and reaffirmed by #28112), application code must never pre-trim model-facing content to dodge a provider limit — the provider's documented limit is a *real protocol boundary*, and the required behavior there is **typed rejection, no partial payload**. This PR implements exactly that:

- `utils/model-errors.ts` — `isProviderContextOverflowError` classifies provider length-rejection shapes (message **and** `responseBody` across the bounded cause/retry chain — the AI SDK masks Cerebras's flat body to a bare "Bad Request"); `PROVIDER_CONTEXT_OVERFLOW` code; diagnostic limit extraction.
- `runtime/planner-loop.ts` — every planner dispatch goes through a recovery wrapper. On overflow it substitutes the **largest completed tool result** with a typed failure text — stating the complete result was rejected whole at the boundary, *never truncated* — and retries once. Second overflow or nothing substitutable → typed `ElizaError(PROVIDER_CONTEXT_OVERFLOW)`. The substitution rewrites **every model-facing projection** of the result (live step, cached `modelHistory` tool message, context event), with warn-on-miss.
- `services/message.ts` + `fallback-reply.ts` — classified overflow becomes `StructuredFailureCause "context_overflow"` with an honest designed reply ("That needed more context than my model can take in one call — try a smaller range…"), character-template overridable. The trajectory **finishes** instead of erroring.

### 2. `CHANNEL_RECAP` (`2a13a183af2`)

A room-scoped complete-transcript action built to the same contract — the counterpart that *generates* the large payloads the boundary owns:

- Room-scoped by construction (no room parameter exists); caller-requested `count`/`offset` with **deliberately no maximum**.
- Exhaustive keyset pagination (`(createdAt, id)` cursor, internal pages of 200) until the requested dialogue depth is satisfied or the store is exhausted; a non-advancing cursor is a typed `CHANNEL_RECAP_CURSOR_STALLED` failure, never a silent partial serve.
- Machinery rows filtered before the count; store exhaustion disclosed in the result; oversized ranges are the dispatch boundary's to reject — this action never bounds what the model may see. A 100,000-message request is served complete or rejected typed; there is a regression test asserting it is **not** rejected by the action.

### 3. Composition with the new budget-driven overflow path (`ad0674cb2df`)

While this branch was in flight, #28125 landed `trajectory.modelHistory` (cached message suffix) and `3dd66ebe3f9` landed the estimation-driven `ProviderResult.overflowText` budget substitution. The final commit reconciles the two mechanisms so they compose (documented on `callPlanner`):

- **Budget path** (upstream): estimation-driven, pre-dispatch, swaps *provider blocks* for their declared lossless retrieval forms. Never touches tool results.
- **This boundary**: rejection-driven, at dispatch — the provider's actual rejection is ground truth for what estimation misses (tool results land after provider composition). Only rewrites *tool results*.

Disjoint targets, ordered stages: the estimator lowers the odds; the boundary is the typed backstop.

## Live proof

Replayed on a production Discord deployment (Cerebras gemma, 131k limit): a 900-message recap request served the complete 853,708-char transcript → dispatch rejected → typed substitution verified verbatim in the retry prompt (`CHANNEL_RECAP result of 853708 characters was rejected at the provider context boundary (limit ~131072 tokens); the complete result was NOT truncated…`) → honest reply offering a narrower range → turn finished, not errored.

## Tests

- `model-errors.test.ts`: classifier matrix over the live Cerebras shape, OpenAI/Anthropic phrases, masked-body, retry/cause unwrap + negatives (ordinary 400s, schema 400s, 429, 500).
- `planner-loop-context-overflow.test.ts`: substitution+retry (asserts the retry messages contain the typed failure and **not** the huge content), double-overflow typed error, no-tool-result typed error, ordinary 400 propagates raw.
- `message.context-overflow-reply.test.ts`: honest reply for raw + typed shapes, metadata, template override.
- `channel-recap.test.ts` (22): registration/exposure gates, structural room scoping vs adversarial params, completeness/chronology across keyset pages, 700-of-750 complete serve with advancing cursors, huge-count anti-cap regression, offset semantics, both cursor-stall shapes, exhaustion disclosure.

Scoped set on this head: **142 passed / 0 failed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean; Biome clean. (Adjacent pre-existing failures at base `6941532900d` are unchanged and enumerated in the final commit message.)

Related: #26780, #28112, #28739 (same problem space — natural recall exceeding provider input boundaries; this PR provides the generic typed backstop at dispatch).

# What changed

- Declares every Contacts, Messages, and Phone interaction as either `agent` or `human`.
- Exposes only `list-contacts`, `list-threads`, and `phone-state` to planner-driven DM and VOICE_DM.
- Keeps contact creation/import, SMS send/role changes, call placement/dialer opening, and transcript persistence human-only.
- Classifies generic renderer state/DOM reads (`get-state`, `get-text`, `list-elements`, `describe-element`, `get-focus`, `get-agent-state`) as human-only on all three views; generic fill/click remain blocked by the absent `agent-surface` grant.
- ADMIN-gates all three view declarations to match their existing private providers.
- Removes caller-selected planner read limits. Contacts and Phone request the native signed-32-bit maximum and return a typed incomplete-read error at that boundary; Messages does the same at its native 500-row maximum.
- Treats Messages SMS-role and Phone device-status failures as typed read failures instead of successful null/false state.
- Makes the Phone provider report native failures and expose explicit error context rather than an empty successful planner result.
- Keys production handler maps from the semantic capability unions and documents the read-versus-human-only contract in each plugin README and byte-identical CLAUDE/AGENTS pair.

# Behavioral coverage

`packages/agent/src/__tests__/native-view-planner-parity.test.ts` uses:

- the production plugin declarations and capability arrays;
- the real view registry, VIEWS action, and view-route implementation;
- an in-process Node HTTP server implementing the route adapter;
- production Contacts/Messages/Phone interaction handlers;
- deterministic mocks only for terminal Capacitor bridge calls.

It is not a production agent-server deployment, real Android transport, live model trajectory, or live device test. Coverage includes DM and VOICE_DM semantic reads, named human-only mutation rejection, every generic renderer read/fill/click bypass rejection, USER-role HTTP rejection, incomplete native reads, native status failures, and production-inventory classification.

Epic: #20726

Stacked on #28786 at exact parent `01a44833fd67bc2537d5d5454e80ba6bd07eaceb`.

## What this does

- Atomically admits the next capture operation through the singleton backup operation lane.
- Binds capture authority to the exact live sandbox generation and immutable activation publication, including its exact node-history occurrence.
- Stamps the catalogue lease plus tenant and node fairness watermarks with one database-clock expiry.
- Supports exact replay and non-shortening renewal while fencing A/B/A caller-token cycles with `claimSequence`.
- Fails closed on sandbox, activation, token, funding, provider, node, or history drift and can skip a poisoned earlier catalogue row.
- Preserves the global lock order and intentionally does not row-lock the immutable activation publication.

## Scope boundaries

This split covers capture admission only. It does not wire the runtime worker, enable feature gates, create nodes, call providers, autoscale, deploy, or touch staging/production. Detached publication, verification, garbage collection, cohort/fairness scheduling, restore, and failover remain follow-up splits.

## Validation

- Admission PGlite: 11/11 tests, 72 assertions
- Global lock-order: 6/6 tests, 54 assertions
- Parent lane lifecycle: 12/12 tests, 109 assertions
- Parent migration: 5/5 tests, 19 assertions
- Cloud shared TypeScript: pass
- Targeted Biome: pass
- `git diff --check`: pass
- Exact-head adversarial review: CLEAN, no P0-P3 findings

Exact head: `22376786a933480fb4bf3b453dc11e62dc7c5996`

## Known limitation

PGlite validates transactional behavior in one embedded session; it is not evidence of real PostgreSQL multi-connection interleavings. That proof remains required before runtime activation.

# Risks

Medium: staging release admission now fails closed on anonymous provider discovery. A broken Steward upstream can stop the release before session cutover; a broken deployed API stops the Pages dependency chain; a broken Pages proxy fails frontend verification. Probes are staging-only, bounded and read-only. Production gate conditions are unchanged. No credentials, provider response bodies or request headers are printed by the verifier or new diagnostics.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds bounded reason-only diagnostics to the existing invalid-provider-response 502 path, preserving its public response and fail-closed caching behavior.
- Adds a staging-only verifier for the canonical Steward upstream and API/Pages proxies. GET validates bounded UTF-8 JSON and the real provider contract; proxy GET and HEAD must return HTTP 200, a JSON media type and the thin-path marker.
- Runs the upstream check before staging session-exchange cutover, API discovery after exact-source health verification, and discovery on both staging custom-domain Pages aliases during frontend freshness checks.
- Protects parser/handler parity, duplicate and dangerous keys, UTF-8/body/depth/container/node limits, flat/nested envelopes, safe error reporting and non-staging rejection with regression tests.

## What kind of change is this?

Non-breaking diagnostics and staging release-safety improvement. No new login method, bot installation, account-creation policy, group ownership or Shared/Dedicated routing behavior.

# Documentation changes needed?

It loudly skips unless an explicit real-PostgreSQL DSN is provided.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds two complementary container-admission regressions:

1. A composed PGlite/API test starts two concurrent POSTs for the same project. Both reach the real atomic repository admission method; both return 201 with the same durable container ID, while exactly one complete readiness/logout/pull/inspect/network/create/start transport sequence executes. A retained distinct-project case with limit one still proves one canonical 402 loser.
2. A real-PostgreSQL repository integration test opens independent sessions. A raw holder locks the organization row with `FOR UPDATE`; two repository calls block on two distinct backend PIDs. One case proves same-project single-flight converges to one row and one `created: true` / one `created: false`; the other proves distinct-project quota serialization yields one commit and one canonical `QuotaExceededError`.
3. The Docker-capable cloud `e2e-tests` lane invokes that PostgreSQL file with a fail-closed non-empty `APPS_TENANT_DB_TEST_DSN` guard, so the lock regression cannot silently self-skip in hosted CI.

Together the assertions pin:

- same-project convergence to one durable ID and one complete provider sequence, plus one exact distinct-project quota loser;
- real independent-session PostgreSQL lock contention at the repository boundary;
- response/project identity bound to the sole durable row;
- one readiness/logout/pull/inspect/network/create/start sequence in the composed API path;
- pending → building → deploying state and node allocation 0 → 1;
- billing snapshot `reserved=1`, `used=0`, `remaining=0`;
- bounded gate release, promise draining, and database cleanup on failures.

This is deliberately not evidence for a Workerd runtime, Hetzner Cloud, a Docker daemon, real SSH, or end-to-end PostgreSQL contention through the HTTP boundary.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvement: deterministic backend regression coverage only.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. The tests document their scope and exclusions inline; no public behavior or operator procedure changes.

# Risks

Medium — this adds the transaction authority used before future backup provider effects. No caller or provider mutation is wired in this Draft.

# What does this PR do?

Adds the exact lifecycle repository over the global backup-operation lane introduced by #28773.

- Claims one globally serialized lane with explicit `claimed`, exact-token `replayed`, and non-mutating `busy` outcomes.
- Requires an explicit primary-DB transaction and binds active proofs to its PostgreSQL XID, so a leaked transaction object cannot recreate authority after commit.
- Issues a monotone `bigint claimSequence` execution fence; A→B→A caller reuse gets a fresh sequence and stale executions remain rejected.
- Atomically stamps tenant and exact source-node occurrence watermarks with every fresh claim.
- Uses the primary DB clock for lease decisions and exact CAS predicates for renew/release.
- Re-locks and re-reads DB state when refreshing a proof; released, expired, superseded, foreign-transaction, or mutated proofs fail closed.
- Snapshots all caller inputs before the first asynchronous boundary and rejects malformed UTF-16, non-canonical UUIDs, invalid lease bounds, and invalid exact-node histories.

The lifecycle proof covers claim/replay/busy/reclaim, atomic rollback, tenant/node fairness receipts, explicit-transaction enforcement, XID-bound proof refresh, mutable-input TOCTOU, ABA fencing, renew/release exact CAS, primary-clock expiry, UTF-8/UTF-16 boundaries, canonical UUIDs, and exact node-history FKs.

Refs #25268 and #22896.49:Tb07,## Summary

Stacks on metering PR #28976 and closes the OS-boundary portion of #26821 before any credentialed real-model stability run is permitted.

- runs the untrusted scenario under a collision-free per-attempt system UID in fresh PID/proc/IPC/UTS namespaces with a read-only host/repository view and only explicit output/runtime writes
- clears the root boundary environment and transports only strict allowlisted child variables through a mode-0600 consumed environment file
- applies owner-scoped IPv4 rules allowing only exact loopback mock/proxy ports, denies all IPv6 egress, drops all capabilities, and enforces CPU/file/process limits
- applies x86_64 seccomp that admits `socket()` only for AF_INET/AF_INET6 and denies AF_UNIX, `socketpair`, x32 syscall variants, and all io_uring entry points
- privately overmounts `/run`, `/tmp`, and `/var/tmp` to remove filesystem Unix sockets and host temporary IPC endpoints
- preserves the stacked endpoint/model/schema/request/token/cost metering proxy plus FD3/HMAC receipt controls
- validates workflow-dispatch model input through an environment value, strict grammar/length check, and quoted Bash argument array
- kills all per-attempt UID processes before removing firewall/ACL/user state; includes a TERM-resistant descendant probe
- disables checkout credential persistence and runs the containment proof before the only secret-bearing workflow step

# Risks

Medium — this defines the fail-closed authority reader used by later gateway consumers. It changes no live route, publishes no managed marker, and remains Draft until the producer contract is available on its base.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds one atomic read-only snapshot for the durable managed marker, registration authority, and TTL activation route.

The route carries the complete nested V1 endpoint envelope, including the canonical `runtimeAgentId`. The reader validates exact shape, generation, immutable server name, canonical runtime UUID, strict ASCII DNS/IPv4 HTTP endpoints with ports 1..65535, canonical SHA-256 content digest, and equality with the durable authority's generation/publication/hash. The runtime identity is content-addressed but is deliberately not compared with the managed sandbox ID; that binding belongs to the consumer in #28656.

The transport contract remains purpose-bound:

- Redis OSS adapters must use `EVAL_RO`.
- Upstash adapters must use `evalRo` with the byte-zero `no-writes,allow-key-locking` shebang.
- The authority reader is not handed a generic mutating `EVAL` capability.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and non-breaking routing foundation.

# Risks

Medium — this introduces a shared resolver but does not yet switch a production consumer to it.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds a fail-closed resolver that keeps the managed sandbox identity and runtime/character identity distinct.

- Managed authority snapshots are keyed only by `managedAgentId`.
- A ready managed endpoint must carry the exact requested `runtimeAgentId`; even a fully coherent and content-addressed route for another runtime is rejected before heartbeat and without legacy fallback.
- Managed routing reads only `server:<endpoint.serverName>:url` and requires the raw heartbeat URL to equal the `registryUrl` inside the content-addressed endpoint envelope.
- A second exact managed snapshot after the non-atomic heartbeat GET rejects revoke/republish races.
- Legacy fallback is allowed only when all managed authority keys are absent, then re-reads the legacy pointer and takes a final managed snapshot before returning.
- Any cutover or pointer change becomes `routing_state_changed`, never a stale route.
- Managed starting/revoked/conflict/unavailable states never consult legacy keys.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and routing consumer foundation.

50:T129d,Closes #29341

## Outcome

Signed-in app and Cloud entry points now activate and persist only the account personal Dedicated runtime. Shared remains the public and connector runtime. The change also closes two control-plane failure modes that could leave accepted Dedicated jobs pending without useful evidence.

## Root causes

- Direct login, /join, and first-run read the rowless personal identity and persisted its Shared response without ever requesting Dedicated activation.
- A deployed provisioning daemon could start without DATABASE_URL, fall through to local/PGlite authority, publish liveness, and poll a different empty queue from the API.
- The compatibility control-plane endpoint ignored PROVISIONING_JOB_LANES.
- Worker deploy preflight required Headscale values from GitHub even though the remote reconciliation contract preserves and validates an existing host key.
- The managed Dedicated canary test omitted the newer group-chat dispatch option, blocking the live step before any agent was created.

## Changes

- Add ensurePersonalDedicatedEliza: server quote, exact activation, bounded readiness/cutover retries, and Dedicated-only return type.
- Route direct login, join, first-run, and stale Shared repoint recovery through the Dedicated ensure boundary.
- Retire Shared-first defaults while preserving legacy recovery plumbing.
- Fail deployed provisioning workers before heartbeat unless DATABASE_URL is a valid remote Postgres URL.
- Apply provisioning job lane selection to the sidecar compatibility route.
- Derive canonical Headscale public URLs and preserve host-only API-key authority until remote fail-closed validation.
- Repair the live canary workflow contract.
- Make the protected warm-pool flag authoritative with a safe false default and exact post-restart host attestation; this does not activate the pool.
- Add packages/cloud/infra/AGENT-RUNTIME-ARCHITECTURE.md with the complete Shared, Dedicated, handoff, Hetzner, warm-pool, DB, queue, and failure-mode report.

## Summary

This PR hardens the existing backup catalogue scheduler authority; it does not
add another scheduler, singleton lane, lock service, or provider path.

- Renews claimed catalogue leases from primary-database time after the
  backup-mutation trigger has returned from any lock wait.
- Renews heartbeat leases from that same post-lock clock while preserving the
  original exact owner, generation, and expiry fence.
- Rejects and rolls back a heartbeat if its original lease expires during
  contention instead of resurrecting it.
- Ports the useful real-PostgreSQL multi-session contention harness from the
  closed duplicate lane onto `claimDueAgentBackupOperations`.
- Proves another tenant progresses while one tenant is trigger-blocked and a
  second operation for the blocked tenant remains unleased.
- Runs the PostgreSQL proof explicitly in `cloud-tests.yml` and requires the
  real harness there.

Exact head: `695db6dc98c9fa2be03c79516026ae451027c6af`

Rebased onto `origin/develop@6a318ad3d12f97703dc6e56dbabd1e21c9d32f1f`
with zero conflicts. The owned commit patch-id is unchanged from the prior exact head.

## Risk and scope

Medium-low. Claim and heartbeat updates perform one additional exact-fence
update inside their existing transaction after trigger contention. A failed
renewal rolls the transaction back. There is no schema change, migration,
global lock, scheduler, provider call, feature activation, or deploy.

- Real PostgreSQL multi-session contention on the identical owned patch before the baseline-only rebase:

the repository through independent holder, observer, and worker sessions. It

lease.

53:T1357,# Relates to

Fixes #29153 — `bun run verify` fails at exact develop tip (`32611dfef8`): `@elizaos/cloud-api#typecheck` reports

# Problem

The replacement-attempt locator contract now requires the node occurrence pair (`nodeIncarnation` / `nodeHistoryId` — see `AgentSandboxReplacementLocatorInput` and the `lockAndValidateReplacementNodeAuthority` match against `dockerNodes.node_incarnation` / `dockerNodes.current_node_history_id`), but `createReplacementAttemptFixture` still built the pre-occurrence locator shape. Because `@elizaos/cloud-api`'s tsconfig includes `../shared/src/db/**/*.ts`, the typecheck task fails.

# Change

The fixture now creates a **real node occurrence** and passes its actual pair into the locator (per the issue's acceptance criteria):

- inserts an `agent_node_incarnation_histories` row (`fleet_kind: "cloud"`, `infrastructure_provider: "hetzner"`, a unique `provider_server_id` within the schema's numeric bound — satisfying the history `shape_check`);
- inserts the `docker_nodes` row with `node_incarnation` **and** the occurrence's `current_node_history_id` both set — satisfying `node_occurrence_shape_check` (both-or-neither), the composite FK `(current_node_history_id, id, node_incarnation)` and the `provider_server_uidx` unique index;
- passes `nodeIncarnation` / `nodeHistoryId` into the locator, so `lockAndValidateReplacementNodeAuthority` (allocated_count > 0) matches.

The required locator contract is unchanged — nothing is weakened or cast.

54:T19e8,Closes #28949

# What

Adds the missing owning test suite for the email-curation decision engine (`plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/email-curation.ts`): 27 deterministic, model-free vitest tests in one new file (`email-curation.test.ts`, +792 lines). No production changes.

# Why each test exists (the regression it detects, and who would see the break)

- **Identity delete-blocking** (VIP contacts, known people, protected senders incl. `@domain` forms, personal domains): a regression in `resolveIdentity`/`detectIdentityEvidence` would let a confident destructive `delete` surface in the `/inbox` bulk-review queue for mail from the owner's boss or family — the consumer is the human reviewing that queue (and `InboxService.curate()`, which feeds it).
- **Content delete-blocks** (security/billing bodies, protected labels, thread conflicts): same consumer; a dropped `blocks_delete` evidence class silently re-enables destructive bulk handling of invoices or starred mail.
- **Degraded-mode cap (0.64)**: a metadata-only (body-less) decision presenting as high confidence would mislead the same review surface; `mode`/`degradationReason` are also pinned.
- **Uncited strong-semantic cap (0.79) + `validateCurationDecisionCitations`**: the high-band citation contract backing the review UI's evidence links.
- **Prompt-injection penalty (-0.08) + `promptInjectionCandidateIds`**: the bulk-review surface's injection-warning row.
- **Policy guards** (`allowDelete:false`, `allowBulkDelete:false`, `block_action`, `force_review` hooks): the LifeOps policy layer's override authority over provisional decisions.
- **Duplicate collapse** (`Message-ID` keys, canonical/duplicate ids) and **ranking** (action weights, 1-based ranks, NaN-confidence total ordering): the aggregation the review list renders.
- **`buildEmailCurationPrompt` untrusted-content fencing + astral-plane integrity**: the prompt handed to the curation model; pins the #24134 "complete model-facing content" invariant (caps removed) against the #28039 surrogate-splitting regression class — a 21,000-code-unit body with 3,000 astral emoji survives verbatim with zero lone surrogates.

# Why no existing test owns these contracts

`test/inbox-curation.test.ts` (from #25639) tests the **InboxService wiring layer** — classifier stubbed, identity/policy reached only through injected hooks — and the NaN/tiebreak comparators. It never exercises context-based identity resolution, the content delete-blocks, the confidence caps, duplicate collapse keys, or the prompt builder. Full analysis in #28949.

# Evidence (test-only PR; all rows N/A except where noted)

- New file suite: **27/27 pass** (`bunx vitest run --config ./vitest.config.ts src/inbox/email-curation.test.ts`, 3 consecutive green runs during iteration).
- Full plugin lane: 223/224 pass; the 1 failure (`reflection.test.ts` surrogate test) and 4 skipped real-runtime suites reproduce **identically on pristine develop** (stash-controlled) — pre-existing, untouched by this PR.
- `typecheck` clean; `biome check` clean on the new file.
- **Mutation battery: 16/16 behavior mutations caught** (VIP delete-block off, degraded cap removed, uncited cap removed, injection penalty zeroed, allowDelete off, allowBulkDelete off, duplicate collapse off, ranking weights inverted, force_review off, protected-labels guard off, security/billing block off, thread-conflict flag off, thread-conflict review evidence off, identity hook ignored, untrusted fence stripped, injection-ids suppressed). One further mutation (provisionalAction's blockedDelete ternary) is provably equivalent at the public API — `applyPolicy` re-demotes delete whenever `blockedActions` contains it — and that layer IS covered.
- Independent review: 3-round reviewer convergence (gpt-5.6-sol via RepoPrompt); round 3 APPROVE after fixing 7 must-fix items (both documented regression classes #25639/#28039 now pinned, delete-path assertion strengthened, allowBulkDelete branch covered, protected-sender forms covered, hook payloads made type-valid, cited-evidence variant added). Round 2's contrary source reading was resolved against the fetched `origin/develop` (the working tree it read is 2753 commits behind; #24134 removed the truncation caps).

## Outcome

The Android Cloud APK now ships real foreground notification and location capability paths instead of stripping them and hiding permission cards.

- Keeps POST_NOTIFICATIONS and explicit user-triggered permission requests.
- Keeps LocalNotifications and schedules immediate notifications without exact alarms.
- Keeps PushNotifications with a Firebase-safe no-config guard. Firebase-less artifacts reject register/unregister with `push-unavailable` instead of crashing the plugin thread.
- Keeps ElizaLocation with foreground coarse/fine permission aliases and real approximate/precise position reads.
- Keeps background location, foreground services, boot receivers, wake locks, exact alarms, and MobileSignals stripped.
- Preserves JIT RECORD_AUDIO behavior.
- Reconciles Capacitor settings/plugin metadata and fixes isolated staging package roots nested under the canonical Java package.
- Adds a Cloud-only black Android splash with transparent white face, followed by one embedded transparent white face-plus-wordmark first-frame lockup.
- Preserves non-Cloud custom Android splash icons byte-for-byte and resolves the Cloud-only logo asset lazily.
- Makes the location bridge contract truthful: `background` is iOS-only; Android is foreground-only and never requests `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION`.
- Composes with #29000 launcher-only logging suppression and opt-in WebView inspection.

5a:T1232,## Summary

This is the narrow secure iOS successor to closed aggregate #27216. It preserves the accepted remote-owner security work while excluding the 62-path Devices & Runtimes overlap that blocked the aggregate.

- Reusable owner passwords are accepted only for HTTPS or local/in-process API bases; plaintext remotes remain pairing-only and the password is rejected before serialization.
- Session-authenticated remote HTTP and SSE use the native Capacitor/URLSession cookie transport rather than cross-site WebView fetch.
- Cross-origin mutations select the CSRF mirror keyed by the request target's origin; a stale same-origin page cookie cannot override the remote token.
- Dedicated bearer-capable Cloud hosts retain streaming WebView behavior.

### What realistic regression does each group detect, and who observes it?

| Pinned contract | Regression it detects | Consumer that observes the breakage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dedicated signing-key rule | QA session tokens signed with `STEWARD_JWT_SECRET` / `STEWARD_SESSION_SECRET` / `ELIZA_SERVICE_JWT_SECRET`, breaking the documented rollback boundary | `staging-session-exchange` + `steward-session` routes; older Steward-only verifiers would accept QA tokens |
| Secret (>=32) / keyId (`^staging-qa-v1-[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,48}$`) shape gates, incl. 31/32 and 48/49 boundaries | Weak/oversized key material or malformed rotation ids silently accepted at boot | same routes; `service-jwt.ts` verification path |
| Enablement conjunction (NODE_ENV=production AND ENVIRONMENT=staging AND flag AND version) | QA bridge left enabled in the wrong environment (e.g. production ENVIRONMENT) | `workers-hono-auth.ts` gate on every request |
| Fail-closed allowlist parsing (missing / `*` / non-UUID / >100 entries throw `StagingSessionConfigurationError`) | A permissive parse turning the QA allowlist into a silent match-all | `staging-session-exchange` route minting |
| Structural binding-window validation (UUID/hex shapes, integer seconds, 5s issuer skew, absolute window within 1h max, `exp > iat`, `exp <= sessionMaxExpiresAt`) — each case also asserts primary storage was NOT touched | Forged or mis-minted bindings accepted without revalidation | `steward-session` route + `inference-session-auth-context.ts` |
| Mint hash-proof (`timingSafeEqualSecret` of presented-key SHA-256 vs current row) | A rotated-away key upgraded into a session via a stale cached row | `staging-session-exchange` mint |
| Credential fingerprint HMAC parity (node:crypto cross-check of the domain-separated HMAC) | Fingerprint domain/algorithm drift making every revalidation mismatch | `validateStagingSessionBinding` consumers |
| App-credential + `agent-sandbox:` exclusions | Operator misconfiguration widening an app/sandbox credential into a full browser session | route eligibility gates |
| Logout-marker semantics (logout at/after mint invalidates; before mint does not) | "Logout stays logged out" invariant broken | `steward-session` route |
| `loadVerifiedStagingSessionUser` identity/org re-match | A relinked Steward identity authorizing a different user post-verification | `steward-session` route handling |

Neighboring suites (`workers-hono-auth.*.test.ts`, `service-jwt.test.ts`, `cron.test.ts`) cover their own modules; none exercise the staging-session binding surface. Detailed in issue #29163.

# Risks

Low. The change is confined to `plugins/plugin-vision` and only rewrites how the
OCR results SharedArrayBuffer is encoded/decoded. The wire layout (reserved
16-byte header, `uint32 length`, `uint32 frameId`, `float64 timestamp`, then
JSON payload at offset 32) is preserved byte-for-byte; only the payload byte cap
changed from a hardcoded 65536 to the real buffer capacity, and the writer now
guarantees `header length == bytes committed`. No public export, route, event,
or cross-package surface changes.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

On the high-FPS screen-vision worker path (`targetScreenFPS > 10` →
`VisionService.initializeScreenVision` → `VisionWorkerManager`), the OCR worker
serializes each frame's combined OCR result to JSON and exchanges it through a
5 MB `SharedArrayBuffer`. Both sides hardcoded a **64 KB** cap far smaller than
the buffer:

- writer (`ocr-worker.ts writeResultsToBuffer`, `MAX_TEXT_LENGTH = 65536`) wrote
  the **true** JSON length into the header but copied at most 65536 bytes;
- reader (`vision-worker-manager.ts readOCRResult`,
  `new Uint8Array(Math.min(length, 65536))`) decoded at most 65536 bytes.

A dense screen of text (code editor, web page, document, spreadsheet — a few
hundred text blocks each carrying text + bbox coords) routinely clears 64 KB.
The JSON was then truncated mid-token, `JSON.parse` threw, `readOCRResult`
returned `null`, and the **entire OCR frame was discarded**. `latestOCRResult`
stayed stale/null, so `getLatestEnhancedScene`, `getReadiness().ocr`, and the
scene description silently lost all recognized text for every dense frame — even
though the header recorded the true (untruncated) length, so the data was
knowably discarded.

The fix centralizes the buffer layout and codec in a new
`workers/ocr-results-buffer.ts` shared by the writer and reader:

- a single `OCR_RESULTS_PAYLOAD_CAPACITY = OCR_RESULTS_BUFFER_SIZE - 32` caps
  both sides (replacing the magic 65536);
- the writer commits **every** payload byte and records a header length equal to
  the bytes it wrote — never larger;
- only when a frame genuinely exceeds the multi-megabyte buffer does the writer
  drop lowest-value block bounding boxes while preserving `fullText`, flagging
  `truncated: true` at that hard resource boundary;
- the reader reads exactly `length` bytes and treats `length > capacity` as an
  explicit typed `ElizaError` (degraded to a `null` OCR result and logged at the
  manager boundary), never a truncated parse.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes silent data loss).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The buffer
codec is an internal worker/manager contract; no configuration keys, exports, or
runtime surfaces documented in the package guide changed.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-vision/src/ocr-buffer-truncation.test.ts` — a deterministic
regression suite that needs **no worker threads, GPU, or display**. It drives
the real, reachable `VisionWorkerManager.readOCRResult` (via reflection, exactly
as `updateOCRCache` calls it on an `ocr_complete` message) and the shared codec
the worker writes with.

Reachability of the fixed path: `service.ts` `initializeScreenVision` (line
~603) constructs `VisionWorkerManager` when `targetScreenFPS > 10` (lines
~609–617); `getLatestEnhancedScene`/`getReadiness` consume the `latestOCRResult`
populated by the fixed `readOCRResult`.

  identically on the clean base commit `3e0f196d9a` before any of my changes
  (verified via `git stash`). They concern detector-fusion top-N caps and an
  eliza-1 prompt token budget — different subsystems from the OCR buffer codec.
  My new test file and `vision-worker-manager-readiness.test.ts` pass.

5e:T49b,## Summary

Security follow-up to merged #28129. This change closes destination authorization and reconciliation races while preserving connector provenance and durable world metadata:

- revalidate durable authorization after live Discord member fetch, and before each provider mutation
- accept only live Discord members in the requester’s verified cross-platform identity cluster
- serialize concurrent template reconciliation per account, guild, and template
- route Discord history backfill through the merge-preserving connector path so manual destination roles survive
- preserve the public executeGuildManagement export for compatibility
- retain exact-overwrite hardening and lossless connector binding union from #28129

Closes #28080.

<h2>Biome CLI v2.5.10</h2>
<h2>2.5.10</h2>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11403">#11403</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/8f7786ff62aef61cf75a91e1604cd380456a60f4"><code>8f7786f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed Astro rejecting JavaScript comments between attributes.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>&lt;div /* block comment */ class=&quot;something&quot;&gt;&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;Component /* c */ client:load /&gt;
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11403">#11403</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/8f7786ff62aef61cf75a91e1604cd380456a60f4"><code>8f7786f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed a bare <code>&lt;</code> in Astro text being treated as the start of a tag, such as <code>&lt;p&gt;5 &lt; 6 and 7 &gt; 6&lt;/p&gt;</code>. As in HTML, a <code>&lt;</code> that cannot open a tag is text and needs no escaping.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11438">#11438</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/3133ffa9ae6beaa3c4464edac60de47d5c6f6426"><code>3133ffa</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/8294">#8294</a>: an Astro expression holding only a comment is no longer reported as a parse error, which also stopped the whole file from being formatted.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>&lt;div&gt;{/* a note */}&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div class={/* a note */}&gt;x&lt;/div&gt;
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11403">#11403</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/8f7786ff62aef61cf75a91e1604cd380456a60f4"><code>8f7786f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/9165">#9165</a>: an empty Astro expression such as <code>&lt;div&gt;{}&lt;/div&gt;</code> no longer fails to parse. Astro renders <code>{}</code> as nothing.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11403">#11403</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/8f7786ff62aef61cf75a91e1604cd380456a60f4"><code>8f7786f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed Astro expressions containing a comment failing to parse.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>&lt;div&gt;{/* block comment */ x}&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;div&gt;{/* only a comment */}&lt;/div&gt;
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11403">#11403</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/8f7786ff62aef61cf75a91e1604cd380456a60f4"><code>8f7786f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Added support for Astro's fragment shorthand.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>&lt;&gt;
  &lt;p&gt;a&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/&gt;
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11403">#11403</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/8f7786ff62aef61cf75a91e1604cd380456a60f4"><code>8f7786f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed an Astro frontmatter block being cut short by a closing tag inside a string or comment.</p>
<pre lang="astro"><code>---
const a = &quot;&lt;/script&gt;&quot;;
// &lt;/script&gt; in a comment
---
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/11403">#11403</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/8f7786ff62aef61cf75a91e1604cd380456a60f4"><code>8f7786f</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Princesseuh"><code>@​Princesseuh</code></a>! - Fixed <code>---</code> being read as an Astro frontmatter fence when markup precedes it. Astro only recognizes frontmatter at the very start of a file, so a file opening with a comment now has no frontmatter, and its <code>---</code> lines are content.</p>
</li>
</ul>

<details>

<blockquote>
<h2>plugin-react@6.1.0</h2>
<h3>Add experimental native React Compiler support (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/vitejs/vite-plugin-react/pull/1419">#1419</a>)</h3>
<p>Add experimental native React Compiler support.</p>
<p>You can use it by installing <code>oxc-transform-react</code> and enabling it via the <code>compiler</code> option:</p>
<pre lang="sh"><code>npm install -D oxc-transform-react
</code></pre>
<pre lang="js"><code>import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import react from '@vitejs/plugin-react'
<p>export default defineConfig({<br />
plugins: [<br />
react({ compiler: true })<br />
]<br />
})<br />
</code></pre></p>
</blockquote>
