Updates `verdaccio` from 6.9.2 to 6.10.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/verdaccio/verdaccio/releases">verdaccio's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v6.10.0</h2>
<h3>Minor Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p>51c2733: Expose the optional legacy authentication cache for Verdaccio 6.x through <code>server.legacyAuthCache</code>.</p>
<p>This feature is intended for performance-sensitive installations that still use legacy bearer tokens. When enabled, Verdaccio caches successful legacy token authentication results for a short period of time, so repeated requests using the same token do not need to run password verification through the authentication plugin every time. Concurrent requests for the same legacy token can also share the same in-flight authentication result.</p>
<p>The cache is disabled by default, so existing installations keep their current authentication behavior unless they explicitly opt in. Basic authentication is not cached. If the cache is enabled, changed or revoked credentials may remain valid until the cached entry expires.</p>
<p>Enable it in <code>config.yaml</code>:</p>
<pre lang="yaml"><code>server:
  legacyAuthCache:
    enabled: true
    ttlMs: 15000
    maxEntries: 1000
</code></pre>
<p>Options:</p>
<ul>
<li><code>enabled</code>: enables the legacy token authentication cache. Default: <code>false</code>.</li>
<li><code>ttlMs</code>: time in milliseconds before a cached validation expires. Default: <code>15000</code>.</li>
<li><code>maxEntries</code>: maximum number of cached legacy tokens. Default: <code>1000</code>.</li>
</ul>
<p>See <a href="https://redirect.github.com/verdaccio/verdaccio/pull/6147">#6147</a> and the original 8.x backport in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/verdaccio/verdaccio/pull/6143">#6143</a>.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2>v6.9.3</h2>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p>3c8f391: Reject wildcard characters in package and tarball path validation.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>ebc08ba: Update verdaccio dependencies to the <code>latest</code> npm dist-tag (<code>@verdaccio/ui-theme</code> tracks <code>next-9</code>):</p>

It also includes the <code>@verdaccio/ui-theme</code> update containing the homepage action hover fix from <a href="https://redirect.github.com/verdaccio/verdaccio/pull/6135">#6135</a> by <a href="https://github.com/pranshuchittora"><code>@​pranshuchittora</code></a>.</p>

# Relates to

Supersedes #29191, #29193, #29231, #29241, #29246 — single class-wide repair for per-chunk UTF-8 decoding in `packages/scripts`. Closes #29237 and #29243 where applicable.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

- [x] This PR targets `develop` and is rebased onto the latest `origin/develop`
      with zero conflicts (`git fetch origin && git rebase origin/develop`).
- [x] `bun install` and `bun run verify` were run after sync, or any failure is
      recorded below with the exact unrelated blocker.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the change works without reading the code, from the
      evidence attached below.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased/merged onto the latest `origin/develop`; zero conflicts.
- [x] `bun run verify` passes post-sync, or the exact unrelated blocker is
      documented in **Known gaps / failures** below.

# Risks

Low. Isolated to `packages/scripts` stream decoding; no wire format, API, or storage change. Missing flush would drop trailing bytes — covered by `decoder.end()` on stream `end` and on forced termination.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Replaces per-chunk `chunk.toString()` / `chunk.toString("utf8")` decoding with `StringDecoder("utf8")` per stream across every remaining `packages/scripts` consumer, plus the adjacent surrogate-pair truncation variant in `captured-test-output.mjs`. Decoders use `decoder.write(chunk)` per `data` event and `decoder.end()` on `end`/`close` and on forced-termination/finish paths.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes — correct handling of multibyte UTF-8 split across stream chunks.

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

Five one-file PRs (#29191, #29193, #29231, #29241, #29246) each fixed one file with the same StringDecoder treatment. Per maintainer precedent ("Closing as subsumed by canonical broader authorization repair #28192") a class-wide sweep is required. This PR collapses the five into one sweep covering all 8 FIX files in `packages/scripts`.

**Root cause:** per-chunk `chunk.toString("utf8")` decodes each Buffer in isolation. A multibyte UTF-8 sequence (e.g., `🌟` = `F0 9F 8C 9F`) split across two `data` events is decoded as two incomplete sequences, each replaced with `U+FFFD` (`�`), corrupting output and any downstream parsing that depends on it.

**Affected boundary:** consumers are `run-with-flake-retry` (flake signature matching), `run-live-test-with-artifacts` (bundle `stdout`/`stderr` + trajectory events + viewer), `run-all-tests` (FAIL file inventory via `appendCapturedTestOutput`), `test-cloud-run` (watchdog stdout/stderr classification + `supervisorStatus`), `dev-health-check` (`appendLog` health log analysis), `run-turbo` (WINDOWS_PROCESS_INIT_CRASH carrier scan), `test-console` runner (per-task tail + `chunk` log event), and `captured-test-output` truncation. Loss is a silent `�` replacing the split character, breaking file-name detection, marker detection, and log fidelity.

**Why no higher-level test owns this:** existing suites assert ASCII-only paths, success/failure detection via `PASS`/`FAIL` lines, and marker detection via ASCII `WINDOWS_PROCESS_INIT_CRASH`. No existing suite exercises a multibyte character split across two stream chunks. Verified by running `bun test packages/scripts/__tests__/` on `origin/develop` — all suites pass without ever writing a split `F0 9F` / `8C 9F` sequence.

### Sweep table — `chunk.toString` hits on `origin/develop` (11 files, 18 hits)

| file | verdict | reason |
|------|---------|--------|
| `packages/scripts/dev-health-check.mjs:330` | FIX | child stdout/stderr logged via `appendLog`; split corrupts health-check log analysis and failure signatures |
| `packages/scripts/run-all-tests.mjs:1247,1257` | FIX | captured output for FAIL/PASS classification; split corrupts `FAIL src/🌟.test.ts` file inventory extraction |
| `packages/scripts/run-live-test-with-artifacts.mjs:335,341` | FIX | live-artifact bundle `stdout`/`stderr` + trajectory events; split corrupts viewer and `report.stdout` bytes |
| `packages/scripts/run-turbo.mjs:279` | FIX | carrier scan for `WINDOWS_PROCESS_INIT_CRASH` marker on turbo stdout/stderr; split before marker still corrupts surrounding diagnostics |
| `packages/scripts/run-with-flake-retry.mjs:42` | FIX | tail signature match for flake retry gate; split corrupts regex match across retained 256 KiB tail |
| `packages/scripts/test-cloud-run.mjs:742,754,766` | FIX | watchdog classification + `supervisorStatus` drain; split corrupts stdout/stderr accumulation and release gating |
| `packages/scripts/test-console/lib/runner.mjs:235,240` | FIX | per-task tail (16 KiB) + `chunk` log event emission; split corrupts tail classification and console display |
| `packages/scripts/lib/captured-test-output.mjs:35,51` | FIX | adjacent variant: `String(chunk)` then `next.slice` truncation severs surrogate pair at `MAX_CAPTURED_OUTPUT_CHARS` boundary |
| `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-github.integration.test.ts:154` | NOT-A-DEFECT | server `for await (chunk of request)` assembling JSON body; payload is ASCII `{"key":"value"}` |
| `packages/scripts/__tests__/release-verdaccio.integration.test.ts:166,169` | NOT-A-DEFECT | readiness polling `logs` for local Verdaccio; ASCII `npm config`/`http://` lines |
| `packages/scripts/__tests__/run-live-test-with-artifacts.test.ts:335` | NOT-A-DEFECT | test harness detecting `"READY"` ASCII marker on runner pipe; not production bundle path |
| `packages/scripts/__tests__/test-cloud-run.test.mjs:778,944` | NOT-A-DEFECT | test scaffolding capturing `supervisorControl` stream `"release\n"` / `"0\n"` for assertions; not production watchdog path |

Plus the adjacent surrogate-slice file `packages/scripts/lib/captured-test-output.mjs` (not in `chunk.toString` grep but same data-loss class via `next.slice`).

Before: `git grep -c 'chunk\\.toString' origin/develop -- packages/scripts` → 11 files, 18 hits (9 lines above plus `captured-test-output` adjacent)
After:  `git grep -c 'chunk\\.toString' HEAD -- packages/scripts` → 4 files, 6 hits (only __tests__ NOT-A-DEFECT; production files eliminated). Working-tree `git grep -n 'chunk\\.toString' -- packages/scripts` shows only the 4 NOT-A-DEFECT files.

Sibling `\.toString("utf8")` in production eliminated; remaining hits are `Buffer.concat(...).toString("utf8")` at exit (single-shot, not per-chunk) and test expectations `buffer.toString("utf8")` at `test-cloud-run.test.mjs:140`.

## Summary

Adds a contract-pinning suite (`packages/core/src/runtime/__tests__/addressed-to-resolution.test.ts`, 248 lines, additive-only) for the resolution + persistence half of the addressed-to pipeline in `packages/core/src/runtime/addressed-to.ts`:

- `resolveAddressedTargets` — Stage-1 `addressedTo` tag → room-entity UUID resolution: well-formed UUID fast path (deduplicated, **zero** room lookups — asserted via a lookup counter), case-insensitive participant-name resolution, `@`-handle stripping, `character.name` → `agentId` mapping, unresolvable-name drops, mixed UUID+name passes.
- `applyAddressedTo` — speaker → target `addressed` relationship upsert: edge creation with `addressed`/`addressed:auto` tags and `message_handler_addressedTo` metadata; seeded legacy-edge update (tag dedupe, prior-metadata preservation, `lastInteractionAt` refresh); speaker-target skip (extraction error, never an address target); empty/unresolvable no-ops with explicit empty results.

## Why (regression × consumer)

- **Regression each test detects:** these helpers feed the deliberate-silence gate on every inbound group turn (`MessageService` calls them at `services/message.ts` ~L8937/L9117). A broken resolver silently degrades the gate's fail-safe behavior (a stored handle or mixed tag set stops resolving); a broken upsert corrupts the `addressed` relationship graph the gate and relationship UI read (duplicate edges on repeat addresses, lost legacy metadata on update, speaker self-edges from Stage-1 extraction errors).
- **Consumer:** `DefaultMessageService` (`applyAddressedTo` persistence task + gate resolution on every group turn) and anything reading `addressed` relationship edges.
- **Why no existing test owns this:** symbol-grep across `*.test.ts` on develop shows zero references to `resolveAddressedTargets`/`applyAddressedTo`. The gate functions themselves ARE owned by the pre-existing `addressed-to.test.ts` (#9874 suite) — this PR deliberately does not touch that file (scope correction noted on the issue).

63:T1819,Closes #28995

## Summary

Adds a `bun:test` suite pinning the sign-side behavior of the voice-model catalog service (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/voice-model-catalog.ts`). `GET /api/v1/voice-models/catalog` is public and unauthenticated — the base64 Ed25519 signature in `X-Eliza-Signature` is the trust root, and the device-side updater verifies the exact response text before parsing. Until now, no test in the repository exercised this module: the catalog route has no tests, and the device-side updater (`plugin-local-inference`) is tested only against its fallback sources (GitHub releases / HuggingFace), never against signatures produced by this signer.

## What regression each test detects

- **Body shape pin** (`schema` literal, ISO `generatedAt` from the injected clock, full version list, fingerprint passthrough): the served body is what devices parse; a `generatedAt` epoch-ms regression, a truncated version list, or a schema rename would change what every paired device accepts.
- **Signature interop** (key pair generated by `node:crypto`, seed re-derived from its PKCS8 DER): proves the module's RFC 8410 seed wrapping matches Node's own key encoding and that `X-Eliza-Signature` round-trips through WebCrypto import/verify — a drift in the PKCS8 prefix or sign input breaks this.
- **Exact-bytes contract** (single flipped body byte fails verification; different-seed signature fails): the route signs `JSON.stringify(body)` and the updater verifies the raw response text — any change that signs different bytes than the response carries would brick devices silently.
- **Fail-closed key validation** (31/33-byte and empty seeds rejected before signing; wrong-length public keys rejected in `fingerprintPublicKey`): an unconfigured or malformed key must fail loudly (the route maps this to 503), never sign with a truncated key.
- **Decode-boundary documentation** (lenient `Buffer.from(...,'base64')` decode: an appended invalid character is dropped, decoded length is the gate): makes any future move to strict decoding a visible test change rather than a silent behavioral shift.

## Which consumer observes a breakage

`packages/cloud/api/v1/voice-models/catalog/route.ts` serves this catalog to paired devices; `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/voice-model-updater.ts` (device side) verifies the signature before parsing. A signing regression surfaces as devices hard-rejecting the catalog (no voice-model updates), with no server-side error signal — which is exactly why it needs a CI-level pin.

## Why no existing higher-level test owns this

Before this PR, grep across the repository's test corpus returned zero hits for `signVoiceModelCatalog` / `buildVoiceModelCatalogBody` / `fingerprintPublicKey`. `X-Eliza-Signature` does appear in `plugin-local-inference/__tests__/services/voice-model-updater.test.ts`, but those tests build their own key pairs and fixtures rather than signatures produced by this module, so no existing test exercised this signer. The route is a Cloudflare Worker route with no route-level test.

## Verification

- `bun test src/lib/services/__tests__/voice-model-catalog.test.ts` — 11 pass / 0 fail at afca294bb3 (10 at the original head).
- Mutation controls (module edited, suite re-run, restored): seed-length gate off-by-one → 3 failures; sign-skewed-body (`bodyText + " "`) → 1; fingerprint length-gate drop → 1; schema literal drift → 1; `generatedAt` epoch-ms → 1; PKCS8 OID byte drift → 3. Two DER outer-length byte mutations are not caught (Bun's WebCrypto tolerates them — not observable behavior).
- Response-push RED control: replacing `fingerprintPublicKey` with a passthrough (`Buffer.byteLength` length check + `return` the input) fails exactly the canonicalization test (1 failure / 10 pass) — the new assertions distinguish the implementation from the passthrough refactor.
- Isolation control: `payment-request-settlement.pglite.test.ts` passes 13/13 with and without this file present (this suite uses no database).
- `biome check` clean; `bun run --cwd packages/cloud/shared typecheck` shows only the pre-existing `tsconfig.json` TS5103 (`--ignoreDeprecations`), identical with and without this diff.

## Review response (afca294bb3)

All findings from @sarah-pump-ai's review addressed — see the reply comment for the finding → fix → evidence table.

Test-only change plus comment corrections; no production code behavior touched.

<!-- evidence-head:65657c56adb27166b6ea01bac28a61d56d55dcf7 -->65:T1d27,# Relates to

Closes #29212

test(ui): pin Model Hub view-model helper contracts (hub-utils)

## What realistic regression does each test detect?

`packages/ui/src/components/local-inference/hub-utils.ts` is the pure view-model layer every Model Hub surface renders from (ModelHubView, ModelCard, DownloadProgress, DownloadQueue, ActiveModelBar, FirstRunOffer, HardwareBadge). Its header states it was "kept separate from components so they can be covered by unit tests without a DOM" — but no test file existed anywhere in the repo, and no higher-level suite imports these helpers. Each group pins an observable UI contract:

- **formatEta** — download-ETA rendering buckets. A boundary regression renders every download ETA in the wrong bucket (e.g. a "60s" that should be "1m 0s", or hour-long downloads labeled as minutes). Pins the `Math.ceil` bucket-crossing semantics (59_400ms → "1m 0s"; 3_599_999ms → already "1h 0m") and the empty-string sink for null/0/non-finite ETAs.
- **progressPercent** — the progress bar must never render negative or >100% when a server reports a bad Content-Length (`total <= 0`) or the job over-reports received bytes. Pins the clamp, the guard, and rounding.
- **findInstalled** — install detection. External installs surface as `external-<origin>-<hash>` ids, so the basename-of-path fallback against the catalog gguf filename is the ONLY thing keeping the hub from showing "Download" on a model the user already installed (the documented contract in the module header). Pins exact-id precedence, POSIX and Windows separators, case-insensitive matching, and that mid-path suffixes (`.gguf.bak`) do NOT match.
- **groupByBucket** — hub section rendering iterates all four buckets; a regression that drops empty-bucket keys or reorders them breaks the section layout. Pins key set + order + input-order preservation.
- **displayModelName** — curated-label precedence over a conflicting `displayName`, the `<base>-drafter` label derivation, and the displayName/id fallback chain.
- **bucketLabel / fitLabel** — the user-facing tier and fit copy for every variant (distinctness pinned).
- **computeFit** — delegates to the real `assessCatalogModelFit`; pins fits/tight/wontfit thresholds (70%/90% of effective memory) and the minRam-alone gate with a deterministic CPU-only desktop probe (effective = totalRamGb × 0.5).
- **formatBytes** — hub byte formatting: plain bytes, KiB decimals, the unit-promotion guard (1024²−1 must render "1.0 MB", not the impossible "1024.0 KB"), and the em-dash unknown label for null/negative.
- **findDownload / findCatalogModel** — id lookups incl. miss shapes.

## Which consumer observes the breakage?

The seven Model Hub components above render these values directly: DownloadProgress/DownloadQueue display `formatEta` and `progressPercent` for every active download; ModelCard and ModelHubView decide "installed vs downloadable" via `findInstalled` and group/label via `groupByBucket`/`bucketLabel`/`fitLabel`/`computeFit`; ActiveModelBar shows `displayModelName`.

## Why doesn't an existing higher-level test own this?

The `services/local-inference` suites cover the service layer (catalog, recommendation, readiness, downloader, token-tree) and never import `hub-utils`; the only tests under `components/local-inference` are two hooks (`useDeviceBridgeStatus`, `useHomeModelStatus`) and a stories smoke test. Verified by repo-wide import search at develop tip `a782460787`.

## Proof

- 30/30 new tests green; full local-inference tree 19 files / 207 tests green at the rebased head (develop drift in the interim commits touches none of these files — empty diff for both local-inference dirs).
- Mutation batteries (behavior flips, not literal edits): 18/18 caught — clamp removal, total-guard inversion, both ETA bucket boundaries (< → <=), basename-fallback drop, path-side case-folding drop, empty-bucket key drop, drafter branch disable, rounding drop, null-sink change, basename anchor loosening, bucket-label map emptied, fit-label branch drop, computeFit inversion, unknown-label change, catalog-lookup identity drop, curated-precedence flip.
- `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0 with the suite included (fixtures use `satisfies` with complete required fields — no `as` casts); `biome check` clean.
- Independent reviewer (RepoPrompt, gpt-5.6-sol): 3 rounds — r1 REQUEST_CHANGES (4 must-fix: uncovered exports, vacuous curated test, unpinned case-insensitivity, `as`-cast fixtures), r2 REQUEST_CHANGES (1 must-fix: residual `as ModelBucket[]`), r3 **APPROVE** at the cast-free head.

Low. Test consolidation only. Unifies overlapping colocated and nested `__tests__/` suites in `@elizaos/core`, preserving 100% of unique assertions and granular boundary tests across all 5 pairs while eliminating redundant executions.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Consolidates five duplicate test suite pairs in `@elizaos/core`:
1. `src/build-variant.test.ts`: Retained colocated suite as canonical, merged caching test (5 original + 1 migrated = 6 tests); deleted `src/__tests__/build-variant.test.ts`.
2. `src/app-registry.test.ts`: Retained colocated suite, merged defensive copy, mutation isolation, and slug replacement assertions (3 original + 2 migrated = 5 tests); deleted `src/__tests__/app-registry.test.ts`.
3. `src/security/outbound-envelope-guard.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite with envelope stream safety, merged `reportOutboundEnvelopeBlock` bounded preview test (10 original + 1 migrated = 11 tests); deleted `src/security/__tests__/outbound-envelope-guard.test.ts`.
4. `src/messaging/interactions/callback.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite, added precise 60/61 byte boundary and 15/16 emoji unicode byte measurement assertions (11 original + 7 migrated = 18 tests); deleted `src/messaging/interactions/__tests__/callback.test.ts`.
5. `src/services/channel-topics.test.ts`: Retained canonical colocated suite, merged full LRU, hydration, persistence, and `matchTopicRooms` cross-channel search tests (3 original + 19 migrated = 20 tests, 60 total); deleted `src/services/__tests__/channel-topics.test.ts`.

**Regression detected:** Redundant duplicate suites that split test coverage, increase CI execution latency, and create desynchronization risk across `@elizaos/core`.
**Consumer who observes it:** CI pipeline and contributors maintaining build variant, app registry, security guards, callback codec, and channel topics.
**Why no higher-level test owns this:** The unit suites directly test pure variant resolution, registry singleton, envelope guard, codec byte measurement, and LRU/persistence algorithms; existing higher-level integration tests do not exhaustively exercise each granular boundary condition.

68:T1c12,Closes #28943

## What this adds

A contract-pinning vitest suite for `redactWindowTitle` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/activity-profile/redactor.ts`) — the PII redaction boundary that strips emails, phone numbers, and card PANs from every window title the T8d activity tracker reports (`activity-tracker-reporting.ts:131` calls it on each captured title before aggregation leaves the process). No test in the repository imported this module before this PR.

## What regression each test detects, and who would observe it

| Pinned contract | Regression it catches | Consumer that observes the breakage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CC-before-phone precedence (16-digit PAN redacts whole, incl. phone-shaped `415-496-0148-2211`) | Reordering the replace passes partially matches PANs as phone fragments → PAN digits survive | Activity report consumers: aggregated app-usage titles served to the personal-assistant stack (`aggregateByApp` → report) would carry raw card numbers from e.g. a bank-site window title |
| 13–19 digit gate (12-digit run passes through; 13 and 19 redact) | Off-by-one in either bound redacts invoice numbers or admits 12-digit PAN-like runs | Same activity-report surface — either mangled benign titles or leaked near-PAN digit runs |
| Email redaction (incl. punctuation-bearing local parts) | Dropped/weakened email pattern | Owner emails from mail-client window titles leak into reports |
| Phone forms: E.164, `(415) 496-0148`, `415.496.0148`, `+1 415 496 0148`; 7-digit pass-through | Pattern regressions per form | Phone numbers from messaging/call-screen titles leak |
| Global replacement: 2 emails + 2 phones + 2 PANs all redact in one title | A lost `g` flag degrades to first-match-only → every later occurrence leaks | Multi-PII titles (the common case for a cluttered tab bar) |
| Adjacent-phone quirk (`"4154960148 4154960149"` → `[redacted-cc]9`) | Unconscious change to the greedy-CC/phone interaction | Documents privacy-safe over-redaction so any change to the shape is deliberate |
| Benign pass-through (timestamps, room numbers, 4–6 digit codes unchanged) | Over-redaction drift that would make reports useless | Report readability degrades silently |
| `null`/`undefined` → `null` | Crash on missing titles would break the reporting loop | `aggregateByApp` throws on every interval with a null native title |

## Why no existing higher-level test owns this contract

Repo-wide grep at develop `3763af0215` shows no test file imports `redactWindowTitle` or `activity-profile/redactor`. The `activity-profile/` directory's suites cover the planner, focus sessions, presence bridge, proactive worker, and service pagination — the reporting boundary and its redactor were unowned. The redactor is a pure function with three regex passes; there is no lower level beneath it that could catch these regressions instead.

## Verification

- `npx vitest run --config vitest.config.ts src/activity-profile/redactor.test.ts` — **23/23 pass**; full `src/activity-profile/` directory — **87/87 pass** (7 files).
- Mutation verification on the module (restored byte-identical after each):
  - swap CC/EMAIL/PHONE replace precedence → 1 test red;
  - remove the EMAIL pass → 4 tests red (corrected 2026-08-26: the original count of 3 was low by one, confirmed by re-running the mutation — thanks to @ngngocnhan1997-hub for catching it);
  - run the phone pass before the CC pass → 2 tests red (corrected 2026-08-26: originally reported as 1);
  - remove the three `g` flags → suite goes red (module fails to transform; removing only flag characters leaves multi-occurrence tests failing per independent review-agent verification: 2 tests fail).
- `biome check` on the new file: clean. Scoped `tsc --strict` on the test file: clean.
- Branch rebased on `origin/develop` (`3763af0215`) immediately before push; suite re-run green after rebase.

## Review-response update (2026-08-26, head `4dcfbad423`)

Per @ngngocnhan1997-hub's CHANGES_REQUESTED review, this PR is now a **production fix to `redactor.ts` + the suite as regression proof** (the reviewer's own recommended shape). Summary of the module changes; full detail in the [reply comment](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/28960#issuecomment-5429279487):

- The `digitCount` guard is gone: 13+ digit runs redact **whole** (an exempted >19 match can embed a valid PAN next to another numeric field — `4111 1111 1111 1111/2024` leaked under the exemption design).
- Separator class: single `[ -]` → horizontal spaces incl. NBSP, dot/comma/slash + homoglyphs, all `\p{Pd}` dash punctuation, minus, and every invisible/combining mark (`\p{Cf}` / `\p{Default_Ignorable_Code_Point}` / `\p{M}`). Newline still breaks runs.
- `\d` → `\p{Nd}`: localized PAN spellings (Arabic-Indic, fullwidth) redact.
- Single union character class (not alternation) — closes a catastrophic-backtracking (ReDoS) shape found during verification; a child-process probe with a wall-clock deadline pins linear time, red-control-proven.
- Suite: 62 tests (was 23); activity-profile directory 126/126; biome clean; 10-mutation matrix all-red.

The prior old-head sidecar artifact passed real Pixel chat, Home/Calendar/Notes/Home switching, note persistence, relaunch/history, target isolation, and exact installed-hash proof. This newly rebased exact APK still requires one update-in-place Pixel retest before merge because its embedded source identity and APK hash changed; no device was touched by this source/build lane.6e:Tb3c,## Problem — a design mismatch between pairing and WebSocket auth

Device pairing deliberately mints a **revocable machine-session id** as the client's bearer — never the static connection key (#13985) — and every REST route resolves that bearer through the host bridge's session store. Both WebSocket auth paths, however — the in-band `{type:"auth"}` message and the handshake `Authorization` bearer — accepted **only the static token**. The result: every self-hosted paired device connected, presented its (valid, active) machine-session bearer, and lost realtime with a 1008 close when the auth grace period expired. REST said the credential was good; WS said it did not exist.

## Fix — one shared seam, so REST and WS verdicts cannot drift

Both WS paths now resolve a non-static bearer through the **same session seam REST uses**:

- a new host-bridge method `resolveSessionTokenAuthorization` (`packages/agent/src/runtime/host-bridge.ts`),
- implemented in app-core by `resolveSessionTokenRole` — the bearer-session branch of `resolveAuthorizedRouteRole` factored out and shared (`packages/app-core/src/api/auth.ts`), so the WS verdict reuses the exact `findActiveSession` lookup, machine-TTL handling, and fail-closed store-error policy the route gate applies.

The seam is deliberately **narrower** than the route resolver: no trusted-local shortcut, no static-token or embed fallback — a WS session bearer is either an active session or it is rejected.

## Fail-closed contract

Every failure keeps the rejection the client would have gotten before this change: unknown/expired/revoked sessions, a host bridge that does not implement the method, and a session-store failure all keep the **1008 close** (in-band path) or **401** (handshake path). Query-token gating is untouched. On this rebase the session check is composed alongside develop's host `authorizeWebSocket` hook: static token, then session bearer, then host hook — any one suffices, none widens another.

## Live verification

Shipped and verified on a production self-hosted deployment: before the change, every paired remote device's WS closed 1008 at the grace timeout; after it, paired devices hold realtime across reconnects; a revoked or expired session presenting the same bearer is rejected fail-closed.

## Test coverage

- New suite `packages/agent/src/api/server-ws-session-auth.test.ts` — 8 tests covering both WS paths: session bearer accepted (in-band + handshake), unknown/expired/revoked rejected, missing bridge method rejected, store failure rejected, static-token path unchanged.
- Re-ran the app-core auth lane (`auth`, `auth-session-routes`, `auth-store-failure`, `route-auth-policy`, `install-agent-host-bridge`): 77 passed.
- `tsc --noEmit` clean for `packages/agent` and `packages/app-core`.

# Risks

Low. The change classifies three client-side presentation states: ordinary
login, a valid messaging continuation, and missing/expired continuation
recovery. The main risk is misclassifying stale or hostile return targets.
Mitigations include an exact sanitized `/get-started` match, a valid pending
continuation requirement, explicit `intent=launch` precedence, and negative
coverage for CLI handoffs, query-bearing targets, hostile targets, and OAuth
cancellation. No authentication provider, routing, token redemption, or
backend behavior changes.

Migration tradeoff: an OAuth flow begun on an older build with a plain-string stored destination now falls back to `/join`. That is the deliberate fail-closed cost of refusing timestamp-free state that cannot satisfy the ten-minute TTL.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Gives a valid messaging-continuation login a truthful, privacy-safe heading
  and explanation.
- Rejects unversioned or malformed saved destinations that have no trustworthy expiry.
- Keeps both ordinary links and Telegram account claims in the shared messaging journey.
- Gives a missing or expired continuation an explicit recovery next step.
- Preserves ordinary launch login, CLI/device handoffs, and the full sign-in
  provider surface.
- Keeps continuation tokens and messaging metadata out of the rendered DOM and
  final login URL.
- Adds localized copy for all eight maintained Cloud locale catalogs.
- Adds focused unit/component coverage and responsive-browser regression
  coverage for keyboard order, ARIA hierarchy, reduced motion, and unexpected
  auth/onboarding mutations.

Bug fix (non-breaking).

On August 26, 2026, the ElizaOS team focused on hardening the framework’s cloud infrastructure and mobile stability while significantly improving UI modularity. The project also saw a massive influx of pull requests aimed at ensuring Unicode safety and surrogate-pair integrity across the codebase.

## ✅ Completed Work

### Cloud Infrastructure & Security
*   **Multi-Principal Consent:** Implemented `all_adults` shared consent mode for cloud groups to enhance security ([elizaos/eliza#28361](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/28361)).
*   **Session & Payment Management:** Improved Redis key invalidation on logout and added gated crypto payment confirmations ([elizaos/eliza#28712](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/28712), [elizaos/eliza#29275](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29275)).

### UI Modularity & App Experience
*   **Plugin-Based Architecture:** Refactored UI views into plugins to enable lazy loading and cleaner app architecture ([elizaos/eliza#29151](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29151)).
*   **Navigation & Design:** Standardized launcher icons and improved navigation determinism for agent-aware local views ([elizaos/eliza#29064](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29064), [elizaos/eliza#29335](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29335), [elizaos/eliza#29061](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29061), [elizaos/eliza#29152](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29152)).

### Mobile & Android Hardening
*   **Build Stability:** Hardened the LP3 Android build profile with trusted remote-agent fallbacks and strict color policy enforcement ([elizaos/eliza#29098](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29098)).
*   **Authentication & Permissions:** Streamlined Android Cloud authentication recovery flows and enabled foreground location/notification capabilities ([elizaos/eliza#29282](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29282), [elizaos/eliza#29020](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/29020)).
